<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed <i>C</i> &gt; 0, we prove that a positive proportion of consecutive prime gaps exceed $`C\log p`$, where <i>p</i> is the smaller prime. The proportion is bounded below for every sufficiently large initial segment of the prime sequence, with a constant depending on <i>C</i>. It follows that the indices at which $`p_n/n`$ increases have positive lower asymptotic density, answering a question of Erdős and Prachar.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed real $C>0$, there are constants $c(C)>0$ and $N_0(C)$ such that, for every integer $N\ge N_0(C)$, $$\#\{1\le n\le N:p_{n+1}-p_n>C\log p_n\}\ge c(C)N.$$
  - Explanation: The main theorem establishes that consecutive prime gaps exceeding any fixed positive multiple of the logarithmic prime scale occur with positive lower asymptotic density across all sufficiently large initial segments of the prime sequence.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-ratios)): The set $$\left\{n\ge1:\frac{p_n}{n}<\frac{p_{n+1}}{n+1}\right\}$$ has positive lower asymptotic density.
  - Explanation: The corollary deduces that the set of positive integer indices at which the ratio of the nth prime to its index strictly increases has positive lower asymptotic density, resolving the question of Erdos and Prachar.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-weights)): For each fixed $\lambda>0$ there is a finite constant $K_\lambda>0$ with the following property. For every $\varepsilon>0$, there are weights $W_X(m)\ge0$, defined for integers $X<m\le2X$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_X W_X&\longrightarrow1, &\mathbb E_X(W_XV_J)&\longrightarrow\lambda,\label{eq:weight-first}\\ \limsup_{X\to\infty}\mathbb E_X(W_XV_I)&\le\varepsilon, &\limsup_{X\to\infty}\mathbb E_X(W_XV_J^2)&\le K_\lambda,\label{eq:weight-primes}\\ \mathbb E_X W_X^2&=O_{\lambda,\varepsilon}(1).\label{eq:weight-second} \end{aligned}$$ The constant $K_\lambda$ is independent of $\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: This proposition guarantees the existence of nonnegative real weights on dyadic integer intervals with unit asymptotic mean, positive first-moment prime detection in the first adjacent interval, bounded second moment for prime counts in the first interval with a constant independent of the suppression tolerance, arbitrarily small weighted prime mass in the second interval, and bounded unmarked second moment.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-adjacent-gaps)): Suppose $h\ge1$ and that for at least $\delta X$ integers $m\in(X,2X]$, the interval $(m,m+h]$ contains a prime and $(m+h,m+2h]$ contains none. Then at least $\delta X/h$ distinct primes $p\in(X,2X+h]$ have a consecutive successor $p^+$ satisfying $p^+-p>h$.
  - Explanation: This combinatorial lemma converts a positive proportion of integer starting points where the first interval contains a prime and the adjacent interval of equal length contains none into a lower bound on the number of distinct primes initiating a consecutive prime gap longer than the interval length, using bounded shift multiplicity.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-weight-moments)): Hold $\lambda$, $k$, and the family $(f_j)_{0\le j\le k}$ fixed. Then, as $X\to\infty$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_XZ^2&\longrightarrow w:=\sum_{j=0}^k\alpha_j\\|f_j\\|_2^2, &\mathbb E_XZ^4&=O_{\lambda,k,\mathbf f}(1), \label{eq:weight-unmarked}\\ \mathbb E_X(Z^2V_J)&\longrightarrow\lambda w, &\mathbb E_X(Z^2V_I)&\longrightarrow v:=\lambda\sum_{j=0}^k\alpha_j \\|f_j+Tf_{j+1}\\|_2^2. \label{eq:weight-marked} \end{aligned}$$ Choose once and for all a real $G\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ with $G(0)=1$ and $G(u)=0$ for $u\ge\tau$, and put $$c_G=\int_0^\infty \|G'(u)\|^2\,du, \qquad C_*=\lambda+\lambda^2c_G^2.$$ For every fixed family as above, $$\limsup_{X\to\infty}\mathbb E_X(Z^2V_J^2)\le C_*w. \label{eq:detector-bound}$$ In particular, $C_*$ is independent of $k$ and of the family $(f_j)$.
  - Explanation: This proposition establishes the asymptotic second and fourth unmarked moments of the signed multidimensional divisor sum, its marked prime moments on the two adjacent intervals, and an upper bound on the detector second moment with a constant independent of the dimension and test-function family.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-small-first-moment)): For every $\lambda>0$ and $\beta>0$, there exists a nonnegative $g\in C_c^\infty((0,\infty))$ such that $$\int_0^\infty g(u)^2\,du=1,\qquad \int_0^\infty g(u)\,du=\sqrt\lambda,\qquad \mu_g:=\int_0^\infty u g(u)^2\,du<\beta.$$
  - Explanation: This lemma constructs a smooth nonnegative compactly supported one-dimensional profile function on the positive half-line with unit squared norm, prescribed integral, and arbitrarily small first moment with respect to its squared density.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-dimension-cancellation)): For every fixed $\lambda>0$, there are families of the type used in Proposition <a href="#prop:weight-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:weight-moments">5</a>, with increasing finite maximum dimension $k$, for which $w\to1$ and $v\to0$.
  - Explanation: This proposition proves that choosing alternating-sign tensor-product profiles truncated to the simplex across a block of neighboring high dimensions drives the unmarked quadratic form to one while cancelling the coupled marked quadratic form to zero.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-mixed-moments)): Put $L=\log X$, where $X$ tends to infinity through integers, and let $\mathbb E_X$ denote the average over $X<m\le2X$. Fix nonnegative integers $j_1,\ldots,j_R$. For each $r$, let $F_r$ be a real function on $[0,\infty)^{j_r}$ admitting a smooth compactly supported extension to $\mathbb R^{j_r}$, and suppose that on the orthant it vanishes outside $$v_1+\cdots+v_{j_r}\le\rho_r.$$ Here the support budgets $\rho_r\ge0$ are fixed. Dimension zero means a scalar, with support budget zero. For positive divisors define $$D_{F_r}(m;\mathbf b^{(r)})= \sum_{d_{r,\ell}\mid m+b_{r,\ell}\ (1\le\ell\le j_r)} \prod_{\ell=1}^{j_r}\mu(d_{r,\ell}) F_r\left(\left(\frac{\log d_{r,\ell}}L\right)_{\ell=1}^{j_r}\right).$$ For $j_r=0$, this means $D_{F_r}=F_r$.* *Let $\Gamma=\{(r,\ell):1\le r\le R,\ 1\le\ell\le j_r\}$, and fix a surjection $\kappa:\Gamma\to\{1,\ldots,t\}$. Write $\mathcal M_i=\kappa^{-1}(\{i\})$. Take distinct integer shifts $a_1,\ldots,a_t$ with $\|a_i\|\le DL^B$, where $D>0$ and $B\ge1$ are fixed, and set $b_{r,\ell}=a_{\kappa(r,\ell)}$. Choose $\delta\in\{0,1\}$. In the marked case take one more shift $a_0$, distinct from these and satisfying the same bound. Put $$(\chi_\delta(m),\mathcal H)= \begin{cases} (1,\{a_1,\ldots,a_t\}),&\delta=0,\\ (\vartheta(m+a_0),\{a_0,a_1,\ldots,a_t\}),&\delta=1, \end{cases} \qquad \vartheta(n)=(\log n)1_{\{n\text{ prime}\}}.$$ Assume $\sum_r\rho_r<1$ if $\delta=0$, and $\sum_r\rho_r<1/2$ if $\delta=1$. Then there is a real constant $\mathcal C$, depending only on the functions and $\kappa$, such that $$\label{eq:mixed-moment} \mathbb E_X\left[\chi_\delta(m)\prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\mathbf b^{(r)})\right] =L^{-t}\left\{\mathfrak S(\mathcal H)\mathcal C +O\!\left(L^{-1/2}(\log L)^A\right)\right\}.$$ Here $A$ is a fixed finite number depending only on $\sum_rj_r$; the implied constant may depend on all the fixed data, but is uniform in the actual shifts. In particular the error is additive when $\mathfrak S(\mathcal H)=0$.* *If each $\|\mathcal M_i\|$ is at most two, the constant is $$\label{eq:mixed-derivative-constant} \mathcal C= \int\prod_r\left((-1)^{j_r}\partial_1\cdots\partial_{j_r}F_r\right) \big((y_{\kappa(r,\ell)})_{\ell=1}^{j_r}\big) \prod_{i:\,\|\mathcal M_i\|=2}dy_i,$$ where $y_i=0$ for $\|\mathcal M_i\|=1$, and each integrated variable ranges over $[0,\infty)$. The zero-dimensional derivative is the original scalar. When $\Gamma$ is empty, take $t=0$ and $\mathcal C=\prod_rF_r$.
  - Explanation: This proposition provides an asymptotic formula for mixed moments of products of smooth multidimensional divisor sums with an optional shifted prime factor, factoring the main term into the prime-tuple singular series and a smooth test-function integral with an additive error uniform in logarithmic shifts.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arithmetic-1)): Assume the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem: for every $A>0$ there is $b(A)>0$ such that $$\sum_{q\le x^{1/2}(\log x)^{-b(A)}} \max_{(c,q)=1}\left\|\psi(x;q,c)-\frac{x}{\varphi(q)}\right\| \ll_A x(\log x)^{-A}. \label{eq:arithmetic-BV}$$ Here $\psi(x;q,c)=\sum_{n\le x,\ n\equiv c\pmod q}\Lambda(n)$, and $q=1$ is included. Fix $0\le\sigma<1/2$, $K\ge1$, and define $$E_X(q)=\max_{\substack{a\in\mathbb Z,\ \|a\|\le H_X\\(c,q)=1}} \left\|\sum_{\substack{X+a<n\le2X+a\\n\equiv c\pmod q}}\vartheta(n) -\frac{X}{\varphi(q)}\right\|.$$ For every $A>0$, $$\sum_{q\le X^\sigma}\mu^2(q)K^{\omega(q)}E_X(q) \ll_{A,\sigma,K,D,B}X L^{-A}. \label{eq:arithmetic-1}$$ The conventions are $\omega(1)=0$, $K^0=1$, and $\varphi(1)=1$.
  - Explanation: Assuming the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem, this lemma bounds the divisor-weighted average over squarefree moduli up to a fixed power strictly below one half of the maximum prime-counting error across residue classes and logarithmic endpoint shifts.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arithmetic-2)): Use the notation and support assumptions of Proposition <a href="#prop:mixed-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:mixed-moments">8</a>; this lemma requires only bounded functions $F_r$ and also allows $B\ge0$ in the shift bound $H_X=DL^B$. Put $M=\|\Gamma\|$ and $\sigma=\sum_r\rho_r$. As in the proposition, assume $\sigma<1$ when $\delta=0$ and $\sigma<1/2$ when $\delta=1$.* *For a squarefree divisor tuple $\mathbf d=(d_\gamma)_{\gamma\in\Gamma}$, put $q=[d_\gamma:\gamma\in\Gamma]$, with $q=1$ when $M=0$. Call the tuple compatible if, for every $p\mid q$, the shifts $b_\gamma$ with $p\mid d_\gamma$ have one common residue modulo $p$. When $\delta=1$, require this residue to differ from $a_0\bmod p$. Set $$\rho_\delta(\mathbf d)= \begin{cases} \displaystyle\prod_{p\mid q}(p-\delta)^{-1}, &\mathbf d\text{ compatible},\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}, \end{cases}$$ and define the finite density sum $$\mathcal A_\delta= \sum_{\mathbf d\text{ squarefree}} \rho_\delta(\mathbf d)\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\mu(d_\gamma) \prod_rF_r\left( \left(\frac{\log d_{r,\ell}}L\right)_{\ell=1}^{j_r}\right). \label{eq:arithmetic-5}$$ Empty products are one. For every $A>0$, uniformly in the allowed shifts, $$\mathbb E_X\left[\chi_\delta(m) \prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\mathbf b^{(r)})\right] =\mathcal A_\delta+O_A(L^{-A}). \label{eq:arithmetic-6}$$ The implied constant may depend on the fixed functions, dimensions, support budgets, $D,B,A$. For $\delta=0$ and $M\ge1$, the error is $$O\bigl(X^{\sigma-1}(1+\log X)^{M-1} \prod_r\\|F_r\\|_\infty\bigr). \label{eq:arithmetic-7}$$
  - Explanation: This lemma reduces the discrete dyadic average of a product of multidimensional divisor sums, optionally weighted by one shifted prime, to a finite density sum over compatible squarefree divisor tuples up to an arbitrary logarithmic-power error uniform in the shifts.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-singular-average)): Fix an integer $s\ge0$ and a constant $K\ge1$. For each positive integer $h$, let $I_1,\ldots,I_s$ be intervals of $h$ consecutive integers, all contained in an interval of diameter at most $Kh$. Then, as $h\to\infty$, $$\sum_{\substack{a_i\in I_i\ (1\le i\le s)\\a_1,\ldots,a_s\text{ distinct}}} \mathfrak S(\{a_1,\ldots,a_s\})=h^s(1+o(1)).$$ The error is uniform in the intervals subject to this condition, with $s,K$ fixed. Here $A_s$ denotes a fixed finite constant depending only on $s$. In fact the normalized sum differs from one by $$O_{s,K}(1/\log\log h)+O_s\big(h^{-1/2}(\log\log h)^{A_s}\big).$$
  - Explanation: This lemma proves that the sum of the prime-tuple singular series over distinct integer tuples drawn from boxes of consecutive integers inside a bounded multiple of the box length is asymptotic to the box volume, with explicit uniform error bounds.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-mixed-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Then there is a real constant $\\mathcal C$, depending only on the functions and $\\kappa$, such that $$\\label{eq:mixed-moment}\n \\mathbb E_X\\left[\\chi_\\delta(m)\\prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\\mathbf b^{(r)})\\right]\n =L^{-t}\\left\\{\\mathfrak S(\\mathcal H)\\mathcal C\n +O\\!\\left(L^{-1/2}(\\log L)^A\\right)\\right\\}.$$ Here $A$ is a fixed finite number depending only on $\\sum_rj_r$; the implied constant may depend on all the fixed data, but is uniform in the actual shifts. In particular the error is additive when $\\mathfrak S(\\mathcal H)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-026.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-mixed-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Then there is a real constant $\\mathcal C$, depending only on the functions and $\\kappa$, such that $$\\label{eq:mixed-moment}\n \\mathbb E_X\\left[\\chi_\\delta(m)\\prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\\mathbf b^{(r)})\\right]\n =L^{-t}\\left\\{\\mathfrak S(\\mathcal H)\\mathcal C\n +O\\!\\left(L^{-1/2}(\\log L)^A\\right)\\right\\}.$$ Here $A$ is a fixed finite number depending only on $\\sum_rj_r$; the implied constant may depend on all the fixed data, but is uniform in the actual shifts. In particular the error is additive when $\\mathfrak S(\\mathcal H)=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-026.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $L=\\log X$, where $X$ tends to infinity through integers, and let $\\mathbb E_X$ denote the average over $X<m\\le2X$. Fix nonnegative integers $j_1,\\ldots,j_R$. For each $r$, let $F_r$ be a real function on $[0,\\infty)^{j_r}$ admitting a smooth compactly supported extension to $\\mathbb R^{j_r}$, and suppose that on the orthant it vanishes outside $$v_1+\\cdots+v_{j_r}\\le\\rho_r.$$ Here the support budgets $\\rho_r\\ge0$ are fixed. Dimension zero means a scalar, with support budget zero. For positive divisors define $$D_{F_r}(m;\\mathbf b^{(r)})=\n \\sum_{d_{r,\\ell}\\mid m+b_{r,\\ell}\\ (1\\le\\ell\\le j_r)}\n \\prod_{\\ell=1}^{j_r}\\mu(d_{r,\\ell})\n F_r\\left(\\left(\\frac{\\log d_{r,\\ell}}L\\right)_{\\ell=1}^{j_r}\\right).$$ For $j_r=0$, this means $D_{F_r}=F_r$.*\n\n\n*Let $\\Gamma=\\{(r,\\ell):1\\le r\\le R,\\ 1\\le\\ell\\le j_r\\}$, and fix a surjection $\\kappa:\\Gamma\\to\\{1,\\ldots,t\\}$. Write $\\mathcal M_i=\\kappa^{-1}(\\{i\\})$. Take distinct integer shifts $a_1,\\ldots,a_t$ with $|a_i|\\le DL^B$, where $D>0$ and $B\\ge1$ are fixed, and set $b_{r,\\ell}=a_{\\kappa(r,\\ell)}$. Choose $\\delta\\in\\{0,1\\}$. In the marked case take one more shift $a_0$, distinct from these and satisfying the same bound. Put $$(\\chi_\\delta(m),\\mathcal H)=\n \\begin{cases}\n (1,\\{a_1,\\ldots,a_t\\}),&\\delta=0,\\\\\n (\\vartheta(m+a_0),\\{a_0,a_1,\\ldots,a_t\\}),&\\delta=1,\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad \\vartheta(n)=(\\log n)1_{\\{n\\text{ prime}\\}}.$$ Assume $\\sum_r\\rho_r<1$ if $\\delta=0$, and $\\sum_r\\rho_r<1/2$ if $\\delta=1$. Then there is a real constant $\\mathcal C$, depending only on the functions and $\\kappa$, such that $$\\label{eq:mixed-moment}\n \\mathbb E_X\\left[\\chi_\\delta(m)\\prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\\mathbf b^{(r)})\\right]\n =L^{-t}\\left\\{\\mathfrak S(\\mathcal H)\\mathcal C\n +O\\!\\left(L^{-1/2}(\\log L)^A\\right)\\right\\}.$$ Here $A$ is a fixed finite number depending only on $\\sum_rj_r$; the implied constant may depend on all the fixed data, but is uniform in the actual shifts. In particular the error is additive when $\\mathfrak S(\\mathcal H)=0$.*\n\n\n*If each $|\\mathcal M_i|$ is at most two, the constant is $$\\label{eq:mixed-derivative-constant}\n \\mathcal C=\n \\int\\prod_r\\left((-1)^{j_r}\\partial_1\\cdots\\partial_{j_r}F_r\\right)\n       \\big((y_{\\kappa(r,\\ell)})_{\\ell=1}^{j_r}\\big)\n       \\prod_{i:\\,|\\mathcal M_i|=2}dy_i,$$ where $y_i=0$ for $|\\mathcal M_i|=1$, and each integrated variable ranges over $[0,\\infty)$. The zero-dimensional derivative is the original scalar. When $\\Gamma$ is empty, take $t=0$ and $\\mathcal C=\\prod_rF_r$.",
      "explanation": "This proposition provides an asymptotic formula for mixed moments of products of smooth multidimensional divisor sums with an optional shifted prime factor, factoring the main term into the prime-tuple singular series and a smooth test-function integral with an additive error uniform in logarithmic shifts.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-mixed-moments",

          "source_id": "PM-026.1"
        },
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arithmetic-1",
        "exact_quote": "For every $A>0$, $$\\sum_{q\\le X^\\sigma}\\mu^2(q)K^{\\omega(q)}E_X(q)\n \\ll_{A,\\sigma,K,D,B}X L^{-A}.\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-1}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-026.1"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-arithmetic-1",
          "exact_quote": "Assume the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem: for every $A>0$ there is $b(A)>0$ such that $$\\sum_{q\\le x^{1/2}(\\log x)^{-b(A)}}\n \\max_{(c,q)=1}\\left|\\psi(x;q,c)-\\frac{x}{\\varphi(q)}\\right|\n \\ll_A x(\\log x)^{-A}.\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-BV}$$ Here $\\psi(x;q,c)=\\sum_{n\\le x,\\ n\\equiv c\\pmod q}\\Lambda(n)$, and $q=1$ is included. Fix $0\\le\\sigma<1/2$, $K\\ge1$, and define $$E_X(q)=\\max_{\\substack{a\\in\\mathbb Z,\\ |a|\\le H_X\\\\(c,q)=1}}\n\\left|\\sum_{\\substack{X+a<n\\le2X+a\\\\n\\equiv c\\pmod q}}\\vartheta(n)\n       -\\frac{X}{\\varphi(q)}\\right|.$$ For every $A>0$, $$\\sum_{q\\le X^\\sigma}\\mu^2(q)K^{\\omega(q)}E_X(q)\n \\ll_{A,\\sigma,K,D,B}X L^{-A}.\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-1}$$ The conventions are $\\omega(1)=0$, $K^0=1$, and $\\varphi(1)=1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-026.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Assume the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem: for every $A>0$ there is $b(A)>0$ such that $$\\sum_{q\\le x^{1/2}(\\log x)^{-b(A)}}\n \\max_{(c,q)=1}\\left|\\psi(x;q,c)-\\frac{x}{\\varphi(q)}\\right|\n \\ll_A x(\\log x)^{-A}.\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-BV}$$ Here $\\psi(x;q,c)=\\sum_{n\\le x,\\ n\\equiv c\\pmod q}\\Lambda(n)$, and $q=1$ is included. Fix $0\\le\\sigma<1/2$, $K\\ge1$, and define $$E_X(q)=\\max_{\\substack{a\\in\\mathbb Z,\\ |a|\\le H_X\\\\(c,q)=1}}\n\\left|\\sum_{\\substack{X+a<n\\le2X+a\\\\n\\equiv c\\pmod q}}\\vartheta(n)\n       -\\frac{X}{\\varphi(q)}\\right|.$$ For every $A>0$, $$\\sum_{q\\le X^\\sigma}\\mu^2(q)K^{\\omega(q)}E_X(q)\n \\ll_{A,\\sigma,K,D,B}X L^{-A}.\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-1}$$ The conventions are $\\omega(1)=0$, $K^0=1$, and $\\varphi(1)=1$.",
      "explanation": "Assuming the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem, this lemma bounds the divisor-weighted average over squarefree moduli up to a fixed power strictly below one half of the maximum prime-counting error across residue classes and logarithmic endpoint shifts.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-arithmetic-2",
        "exact_quote": "For every $A>0$, uniformly in the allowed shifts, $$\\mathbb E_X\\left[\\chi_\\delta(m)\n             \\prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\\mathbf b^{(r)})\\right]\n =\\mathcal A_\\delta+O_A(L^{-A}).\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-6}$$ The implied constant may depend on the fixed functions, dimensions, support budgets, $D,B,A$. For $\\delta=0$ and $M\\ge1$, the error is $$O\\bigl(X^{\\sigma-1}(1+\\log X)^{M-1}\n          \\prod_r\\|F_r\\|_\\infty\\bigr).\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-7}$$",

          "exact_quote": "For every $A>0$, uniformly in the allowed shifts, $$\\mathbb E_X\\left[\\chi_\\delta(m)\n             \\prod_rD_{F_r}(m;\\mathbf b^{(r)})\\right]\n =\\mathcal A_\\delta+O_A(L^{-A}).\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-6}$$ The implied constant may depend on the fixed functions, dimensions, support budgets, $D,B,A$. For $\\delta=0$ and $M\\ge1$, the error is $$O\\bigl(X^{\\sigma-1}(1+\\log X)^{M-1}\n          \\prod_r\\|F_r\\|_\\infty\\bigr).\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-7}$$",

Put $M=|\\Gamma|$ and $\\sigma=\\sum_r\\rho_r$. As in the proposition, assume $\\sigma<1$ when $\\delta=0$ and $\\sigma<1/2$ when $\\delta=1$.*\n\n\n\n\n*For a squarefree divisor tuple $\\mathbf d=(d_\\gamma)_{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma}$, put $q=[d_\\gamma:\\gamma\\in\\Gamma]$, with $q=1$ when $M=0$. Call the tuple compatible if, for every $p\\mid q$, the shifts $b_\\gamma$ with $p\\mid d_\\gamma$ have one common residue modulo $p$. When $\\delta=1$, require this residue to differ from $a_0\\bmod p$. Set $$\\rho_\\delta(\\mathbf d)=\n \\begin{cases}\n  \\displaystyle\\prod_{p\\mid q}(p-\\delta)^{-1},\n       &\\mathbf d\\text{ compatible},\\\\\n  0,&\\text{otherwise},\n \\end{cases}$$ and define the finite density sum $$\\mathcal A_\\delta=\n \\sum_{\\mathbf d\\text{ squarefree}}\n \\rho_\\delta(\\mathbf d)\\prod_{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma}\\mu(d_\\gamma)\n \\prod_rF_r\\left(\n       \\left(\\frac{\\log d_{r,\\ell}}L\\right)_{\\ell=1}^{j_r}\\right).\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-5}$$ Empty products are one. For $\\delta=0$ and $M\\ge1$, the error is $$O\\bigl(X^{\\sigma-1}(1+\\log X)^{M-1}\n          \\prod_r\\|F_r\\|_\\infty\\bigr).\n\\label{eq:arithmetic-7}$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Consecutive prime gap sequence** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Let $p_n$ denote the $n$th prime and write $d_n=p_{n+1}-p_n$.
- **Lower asymptotic density** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For a set $A$ of positive integers, its lower asymptotic density is $\liminf_{N\to\infty}\|A\cap[1,N]\|/N$.
- **Adjacent logarithmic shift intervals** ([source](#sec:counting)): For an integer $X\ge2$ and a fixed $\lambda>0$, write $$L=\log X,\qquad h=\lfloor\lambda L\rfloor,\qquad J=\{1,\ldots,h\},\qquad I=\{h+1,\ldots,2h\}.$$
- **Dyadic uniform average operator** ([source](#sec:counting)): We use the uniform average $\mathbb E_X A=X^{-1}\sum_{X<m\le2X}A(m)$.
- **Normalized block prime weight** ([source](#sec:counting)): Set $\vartheta(n)=(\log n)\mathbf1_{\{n\ {\rm prime}\}}$ and $$V_B(m)=\frac1L\sum_{b\in B}\vartheta(m+b)\qquad(B=I,J).$$
- **Multidimensional smooth divisor sum** ([source](#sec:weights)): For a compactly supported function $F:[0,\infty)^j\to\mathbb R$ and a tuple of distinct shifts $\mathbf b=(b_1,\ldots,b_j)$, define $$D_F(m;\mathbf b) =\sum_{d_i\mid m+b_i}\mu(d_1)\cdots\mu(d_j) F\left(\frac{\log d_1}{L},\ldots, \frac{\log d_j}{L}\right).$$
- **Cumulative profile integral and coordinate-integration operator** ([source](#eq:cumulative-derivative)): Define $$F_j(\mathbf v)=\int_{s_i\ge v_i\ (1\le i\le j)} f_j(\mathbf s)\,d\mathbf s, \qquad F_0=f_0, \qquad (Tf_{j+1})(\mathbf t)=\int_0^\infty f_{j+1}(\mathbf t,u)\,du.$$
- **Signed subset divisor sum** ([source](#sec:weights)): Define the signed sum $$Z(m)=\sum_{j=0}^k\sum_{\substack{S\subset I\\\|S\|=j}}D_{F_j}(m;S), \qquad \alpha_j=\frac{\lambda^j}{j!}.$$
- **Prime-tuple singular series** ([source](#sec:moments)): For a finite set $\mathcal H$ of distinct integers, put $$\nu_p(\mathcal H)=\|\mathcal H\bmod p\|,\qquad \mathfrak S(\mathcal H)=\prod_p (1-\nu_p(\mathcal H)/p)(1-1/p)^{-\|\mathcal H\|}.$$

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Count of consecutive prime gaps exceeding a fixed multiple of the logarithmic prime scale | ≥ | $c(C)N$ | Fixed positive threshold parameter and all sufficiently large initial segment lengths |
| Q2 | Gap threshold constant applied in the proof of the increasing prime-to-index ratio corollary | = | $2$ | All prime indices outside a finite initial exceptional set |
| Q3 | Number of distinct primes initiating a consecutive gap exceeding the block length | ≥ | $\delta X/h$ | Positive integer block length and positive proportion of valid integer starts |
| Q4 | Simplex support cutoff parameter for multidimensional divisor-sum test functions | = | $1/8$ | Fixed coordinate-sum support radius allowing up to six divisor-sum factors below unity and two marked factors below one half |
| Q5 | Asymptotic upper bound on the detector second moment for primes in the first interval | ≤ | $C_*w$ | Fixed scale parameter, dimension cutoff, and smooth test-function family as the dyadic scale tends to infinity |
| Q6 | Number of active consecutive dimension levels in the alternating cancellation family | = | $\lfloor\sqrt k\rfloor$ | Increasing finite maximum dimension in the auxiliary cancellation limit |
| Q7 | Upper bound on the ratio of the marked second-interval quadratic form to the interval scale parameter | ≤ | $\frac2r+\left(\frac rk\right)^2+\frac{4s_g^2}{kd^2}$ | Maximum dimension exceeding twice the profile support endpoint divided by the first-moment slack |
| Q8 | Total support budget ceiling for unmarked mixed moments of divisor sums | < | $1$ | Unmarked regime with zero prime marks and fixed coordinate-sum support budgets |
| Q9 | Total support budget ceiling for prime-marked mixed moments of divisor sums | < | $1/2$ | Marked regime with one shifted prime factor and fixed coordinate-sum support budgets |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-arithmetic-1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arithmetic-1): Combine the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem with prime-power removal, uniform endpoint shift bounds, trivial divisor bounds, and Cauchy-Schwarz over squarefree moduli. -> Logarithmic-power saving for divisor-weighted prime distribution errors uniformly over shifted dyadic intervals.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-arithmetic-2) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arithmetic-2): Expand divisor-sum products over squarefree tuples, apply the Chinese remainder theorem on compatible residue classes, and bound prime-counting errors via the weighted distribution lemma. -> Reduction of unmarked and prime-marked divisor-sum expectations to an exact finite density sum plus negligible error.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-mixed-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-arithmetic-2-2): Apply Fourier inversion to the smooth compactly supported extensions, factor the Euler product into zeta-pole factors and local singular-series factors, and evaluate the pole residues. -> Uniform mixed-moment asymptotic formula separating the shift singular series from the smooth derivative integral.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-singular-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-singular-average): Truncate the Euler product of the singular series at a logarithmic prime cutoff, apply the Chinese remainder theorem on boxes, and bound the large-prime tail product via prime divisors of shift differences. -> Asymptotic mean one for the prime-tuple singular series averaged over distinct tuples in consecutive integer boxes.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-weight-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-singular-average-2): Expand the squared divisor sum over shift subsets, eliminate unmatched shifts via coordinate-face vanishing of complete mixed derivatives, apply the exact prime-deletion bijection on the second interval, and majorize the detector second moment with six divisor factors. -> Explicit quadratic-form limits for unmarked and marked moments of the squared weight and a family-independent detector bound.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-first-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-first-moment): Smoothly truncate the reciprocal function on a wide interval and rescale its amplitude and argument to match the unit square-norm and prescribed integral while driving the first moment below tolerance. -> One-dimensional smooth profile on the positive half-line with arbitrarily small first moment under its squared density.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-dimension-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dimension-cancellation): Form alternating-sign tensor products of the one-dimensional profile restricted by a smooth simplex cutoff across a square-root window of top dimensions, and apply Chebyshev concentration to bound interior and boundary residuals. -> Sequence of finite-dimensional test-function families whose unmarked quadratic form converges to one while the coupled marked form converges to zero.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dimension-cancellation-2): Select and freeze a single finite dimension and test-function family from the cancellation sequence achieving the target suppression ratio, then normalize the squared divisor sum by its unmarked limit. -> Nonnegative dyadic weights satisfying all required first-moment, second-moment, and prime-suppression bounds.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-adjacent-gaps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-adjacent-gaps): Assign to each qualifying integer start the last prime in the first interval, observe that its successor lies beyond the second interval, and bound the preimage multiplicity by the interval length. -> Lower bound on the number of distinct primes in the dyadic range whose consecutive prime gap exceeds the block length.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-adjacent-gaps-2): Apply weighted Cauchy-Schwarz twice to convert the weight moments into a positive proportion of integer starts with a prime in the first block and none in the second, then apply the multiplicity lemma and the prime number theorem. -> Positive lower asymptotic density of consecutive prime gaps exceeding any fixed multiple of the logarithmic prime scale.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-cor-ratios) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-ratios): Rewrite the ratio increase condition as a consecutive prime gap inequality, compare the prime-to-index ratio with the logarithm of the prime via the prime number theorem, and specialize the main theorem. -> Positive lower asymptotic density of indices at which the ratio of the nth prime to its index strictly increases.

Each selected finite family has compact interior support: every coordinate is bounded away from zero by a positive support endpoint of $g$ divided by $k$, and the cutoff forces $S_j\\le\\gamma<\\tau$.\n\n\nThe density $Q_j^2$ has total mass one. Under it the variables $kt_i$ are independent with density $g^2$, so, for $j\\le k$, $$\\mathbb E_jS_j=\\frac{j\\mu_g}{k}\\le\\mu_g,\n \\qquad \\operatorname{Var}_j(S_j)=\\frac{js_g^2}{k^2}\n                                      \\le\\frac{s_g^2}{k}.$$ Chebyshev’s inequality therefore gives $$1-\\frac{s_g^2}{kd^2}\n \\le w=\\frac1r\\sum_{j=a}^k\\mathbb E_j[\\chi(S_j)^2]\\le1.\n \\label{eq:w-concentration}$$ This proves $w\\to1$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: If at least delta X integers m in the dyadic interval from X to two X have a prime in the first block of length h and no prime in the adjacent block of length h, then at least delta X divided by h distinct primes in the shifted range have consecutive prime gap strictly exceeding h because each selected last prime is chosen by at most h integer starts.
- **I2**: The strict increase of the prime-to-index ratio between consecutive indices is algebraically equivalent to the consecutive prime gap exceeding the current prime-to-index ratio, which occurs on a set of positive lower asymptotic density.
- **I3**: When a shifted integer in the second interval is prime and exceeds the divisor-sum support cutoff, every nonzero summand from a subset containing that shift has divisor equal to one in that coordinate, reducing the signed smooth divisor sum via an exact coordinate-deletion bijection to subsets of the remaining shifts with coupled adjacent-dimension test functions.
- **I4**: For every fixed positive threshold C, consecutive prime gaps exceeding C times the logarithm of the smaller prime occur for a positive proportion of prime indices on every sufficiently large initial segment, proved by combining a family-independent detector second moment in the first block with alternating high-dimensional profile cancellation in the second block.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU1**: Inferring a positive proportion of large consecutive prime gaps solely from a positive proportion of prime-free short intervals or from lower bounds on the single largest prime gap. -> A sparse set of exceptionally long prime gaps can cover many integer starting points of empty short intervals without producing a positive proportion of consecutive prime indices with large gaps.
- **AU2**: Applying the uniform mixed-moment asymptotic formula when the total divisor support budget meets or exceeds the Bombieri-Vinogradov and unmarked admissibility ceilings. -> When the sum of support budgets reaches or exceeds one half in the prime-marked case or one in the unmarked case, the arithmetic error sums and non-reduced residue class exclusions in Lemma nine and Lemma ten break down.
- **AU3**: Taking the dimension parameter k to infinity simultaneously with or after the dyadic scale X tends to infinity without uniform error control in k. -> The mixed-moment error bounds in Proposition eight and the fourth-moment bound in Proposition five depend on the fixed maximum dimension k and the fixed test-function family, becoming invalid if k grows with X.
- **AU4**: Extrapolating Theorem one or Corollary two to guarantee threshold exceedance frequencies or increasing value-to-index ratios for general software telemetry streams, arrival logs, or non-prime sequences. -> Arbitrary event streams lack the prime divisor structure, Moebius orthogonality, singular-series box averages, and Bombieri-Vinogradov equidistribution in residue classes that drive the adjacent-interval weight construction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite constructive verification of the Lemma four adjacent-interval prime gap multiplicity bound, the Corollary two prime-to-index ratio equivalence, and the Section three exact prime-deletion identity for smooth divisor sums.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object on stdin with integer fields X, h, n_ratios, m_prime, a_shift, d_cut
# PRECONDITIONS: X >= 2, h >= 1, n_ratios >= 2, h < a_shift <= 2 * h, 1 <= d_cut < m_prime + a_shift, and m_prime + a_shift is prime
# OUTPUTS: canonical JSON object on stdout with qualifying_m, selected_primes, selected_list, gap_primes, max_multiplicity, ratio_inc_count, ratio_gap_match, z_full, z_deleted
# TIME: O(X * h * sqrt(X + h) + n_ratios * p_{n_ratios} + h^2 * d_cut^2) exact integer and rational operations
# SPACE: O(X + h + n_ratios) integers and exact rational accumulators
# ARITHMETIC: exact arbitrary-precision integers and rationals via python-flint (fmpz, fmpq)
# FAILURE: exits nonzero on invalid JSON, violated preconditions, or arithmetic failure
import itertools
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpz, fmpq


def is_prime_int(n):
    return n >= 2 and bool(fmpz(n).is_prime())


def next_prime_int(n):
    k = n + 1
    while not is_prime_int(k):
        k += 1
    return k


def sqfree_divs_mu(n):
    out = []
    for d in range(1, n + 1):
        if n % d == 0:
            facs = fmpz(d).factor()
            if any(exp > 1 for _, exp in facs):
                continue
            mu_d = -1 if (len(facs) % 2 == 1) else 1
            out.append((d, mu_d))
    return out


def solve(cfg):
    X = int(cfg["X"])
    h = int(cfg["h"])
    n_ratios = int(cfg["n_ratios"])
    m_p = int(cfg["m_prime"])
    a_sh = int(cfg["a_shift"])
    d_cut = int(cfg["d_cut"])
    if X < 2 or h < 1 or n_ratios < 2:
        sys.exit(2)
    if not (h < a_sh <= 2 * h) or not (1 <= d_cut < m_p + a_sh) or not is_prime_int(m_p + a_sh):
        sys.exit(2)

    qualifying_m = 0
    selected_counts = {}
    for m in range(X + 1, 2 * X + 1):
        j_primes = [x for x in range(m + 1, m + h + 1) if is_prime_int(x)]
        i_primes = [x for x in range(m + h + 1, m + 2 * h + 1) if is_prime_int(x)]
        if j_primes and not i_primes:
            qualifying_m += 1
            pm = j_primes[-1]
            selected_counts[pm] = selected_counts.get(pm, 0) + 1

    gap_primes = [p for p in range(X + 1, 2 * X + h + 1) if is_prime_int(p) and next_prime_int(p) - p > h]
    max_mult = max(selected_counts.values()) if selected_counts else 0

    primes = []
    cand_p = 2
    while len(primes) < n_ratios + 1:
        if is_prime_int(cand_p):
            primes.append(cand_p)
        cand_p += 1

    ratio_inc_count = 0
    ratio_gap_match = True
    for idx in range(1, n_ratios + 1):
        pn = primes[idx - 1]
        pn1 = primes[idx]
        c1 = fmpq(pn, idx) < fmpq(pn1, idx + 1)
        c2 = fmpq(pn1 - pn, 1) > fmpq(pn, idx)
        if c1 != c2:
            ratio_gap_match = False
        if c1:
            ratio_inc_count += 1

    def f_eval(ds):
        j = len(ds)
        if j == 0:
            return fmpq(1, 1)
        prod = 1
        for d in ds:
            prod *= d
        if prod > d_cut:
            return fmpq(0, 1)
        if j == 1:
            return fmpq(d_cut + 1 - prod, d_cut + 1)
        if j == 2:
            return fmpq(d_cut + 1 - prod, (d_cut + 1) ** 2)
        return fmpq(0, 1)

    i_shifts = list(range(h + 1, 2 * h + 1))
    z_full = fmpq(0, 1)
    for j in range(3):
        for sub in itertools.combinations(i_shifts, j):
            div_lists = [sqfree_divs_mu(m_p + b) for b in sub]
            for tup in itertools.product(*div_lists):
                ds = [t[0] for t in tup]
                mu_prod = 1
                for t in tup:
                    mu_prod *= t[1]
                z_full += mu_prod * f_eval(ds)

    rem_shifts = [b for b in i_shifts if b != a_sh]
    z_del = fmpq(0, 1)
    for j in range(3):
        for sub_u in itertools.combinations(rem_shifts, j):
            div_lists = [sqfree_divs_mu(m_p + b) for b in sub_u]
            for tup in itertools.product(*div_lists):
                ds = [t[0] for t in tup]
                mu_prod = 1
                for t in tup:
                    mu_prod *= t[1]
                ftilde = f_eval(ds) + f_eval(ds + [1])
                z_del += mu_prod * ftilde

    return {
        "gap_primes": len(gap_primes),
        "max_multiplicity": max_mult,
        "qualifying_m": qualifying_m,
        "ratio_gap_match": ratio_gap_match,
        "ratio_inc_count": ratio_inc_count,
        "selected_list": sorted(selected_counts.keys()),
        "selected_primes": len(selected_counts),
        "z_deleted": str(z_del),
        "z_full": str(z_full),
    }


if __name__ == "__main__":
    data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
    res = solve(data)
    print(json.dumps(res, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")))
```

u > hv)]\nmult_bound = max(sel_map.values()) if sel_map else 0\n\nfor p_sel in got.get(\"selected_list\", []):\n    if not sp.isprime(p_sel) or not (xv < p_sel <= 2 * xv + hv):\n        sys.exit(3)\n    if sp.nextprime(p_sel) - p_sel <= hv:\n        sys.exit(3)\nif got.get(\"max_multiplicity\", hv + 1) > hv:\n    sys.exit(4)\nif got.get(\"selected_primes\", 0) * hv < got.get(\"qualifying_m\", 1):\n    sys.exit(4)\nif got.get(\"gap_primes\", -1) < got.get(\"selected_primes\", 0):\n    sys.exit(4)\n\nr_count = 0\nfor k_idx in range(1, nr + 1):\n    pk = int(sp.prime(k_idx))\n    pk1 = int(sp.prime(k_idx + 1))\n    lhs = sp.Rational(pk, k_idx) < sp.Rational(pk1, k_idx + 1)\n    rhs = sp.Rational(pk1 - pk, 1) > sp.Rational(pk, k_idx)\n    if lhs != rhs:\n        sys.exit(5)\n    if lhs:\n        r_count += 1\nif got.get(\"ratio_gap_match\") is not True:\n    sys.exit(5)\n\ndef weight_fn(d_tuple):\n    dim = len(d_tuple)\n    if dim == 0:\n        return sp.Rational(1, 1)\n    prd = 1\n    for dv in d_tuple:\n        prd *= dv\n    if prd > dc:\n        return sp.Rational(0, 1)\n    if dim == 1:\n        return sp.Rational(dc + 1 - prd, dc + 1)\n    if dim == 2:\n        return sp.Rational(dc + 1 - prd, (dc + 1) ** 2)\n    return sp.Rational(0, 1)\n\nshifts_i = list(range(hv + 1, 2 * hv + 1))\nz_ref = sp.Rational(0, 1)\nz_ref += weight_fn(())\nfor idx1, b1 in enumerate(shifts_i):\n    for d1 in sp.divisors(mp + b1):\n        m1 = int(sp.mobius(d1))\n        if m1 != 0:\n            z_ref += m1 * weight_fn((d1,))\n    for b2 in shifts_i[idx1 + 1:]:\n        for d1 in sp.divisors(mp + b1):\n            m1 = int(sp.mobius(d1))\n            if m1 == 0:\n                continue\n            for d2 in sp.divisors(mp + b2):\n                m2 = int(sp.mobius(d2))\n                if m2 != 0:\n                    z_ref += (m1 * m2) * weight_fn((d1, d2))\n\nshifts_u = [b for b in shifts_i if b != ash]\nz_red = weight_fn(()) + weight_fn((1,))\nfor idx1, b1 in enumerate(shifts_u):\n    for d1 in sp.divisors(mp + b1):\n        m1 = int(sp.mobius(d1))\n        if m1 != 0:\n            z_red += m1 * (weight_fn((d1,)) + weight_fn((d1, 1)))\n    for b2 in shifts_u[idx1 + 1:]:\n        for d1 in sp.divisors(mp + b1):\n            m1 = int(sp.mobius(d1))\n            if m1 == 0:\n                continue\n            for d2 in sp.divisors(mp + b2):\n                m2 = int(sp.mobius(d2))\n                if m2 != 0:\n                    z_red += (m1 * m2) * (weight_fn((d1, d2)) + weight_fn((d1, d2, 1)))\n\nif z_ref != z_red or got.get(\"z_full\") != got.get(\"z_deleted\"):\n    sys.exit(6)\n\ntruth = {\n    \"gap_primes\": len(all_gap_p),\n    \"max_multiplicity\": mult_bound,\n    \"qualifying_m\": len(m_hits),\n    \"ratio_gap_match\": True,\n    \"ratio_inc_count\": r_count,\n    \"selected_list\": sorted(sel_map.keys()),\n    \"selected_primes\": len(sel_map),\n    \"z_deleted\": str(z_red),\n    \"z_full\": str(z_ref),\n}\nif got != truth or got != exp:\n    sys.exit(7)\nsys.exit(0)\n",

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object on stdin with integer fields X, h, n_ratios, m_prime, a_shift, d_cut\n# PRECONDITIONS: X >= 2, h >= 1, n_ratios >= 2, h < a_shift <= 2 * h, 1 <= d_cut < m_prime + a_shift, and m_prime + a_shift is prime\n# OUTPUTS: canonical JSON object on stdout with qualifying_m, selected_primes, selected_list, gap_primes, max_multiplicity, ratio_inc_count, ratio_gap_match, z_full, z_deleted\n# TIME: O(X * h * sqrt(X + h) + n_ratios * p_{n_ratios} + h^2 * d_cut^2) exact integer and rational operations\n# SPACE: O(X + h + n_ratios) integers and exact rational accumulators\n# ARITHMETIC: exact arbitrary-precision integers and rationals via python-flint (fmpz, fmpq)\n# FAILURE: exits nonzero on invalid JSON, violated preconditions, or arithmetic failure\nimport itertools\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom flint import fmpz, fmpq\n\n\ndef is_prime_int(n):\n    return n >= 2 and bool(fmpz(n).is_prime())\n\n\ndef next_prime_int(n):\n    k = n + 1\n    while not is_prime_int(k):\n        k += 1\n    return k\n\n\ndef sqfree_divs_mu(n):\n    out = []\n    for d in range(1, n + 1):\n        if n % d == 0:\n            facs = fmpz(d).factor()\n            if any(exp > 1 for _, exp in facs):\n                continue\n            mu_d = -1 if (len(facs) % 2 == 1) else 1\n            out.append((d, mu_d))\n    return out\n\n\ndef solve(cfg):\n    X = int(cfg[\"X\"])\n    h = int(cfg[\"h\"])\n    n_ratios = int(cfg[\"n_ratios\"])\n    m_p = int(cfg[\"m_prime\"])\n    a_sh = int(cfg[\"a_shift\"])\n    d_cut = int(cfg[\"d_cut\"])\n    if X < 2 or h < 1 or n_ratios < 2:\n        sys.exit(2)\n    if not (h < a_sh <= 2 * h) or not (1 <= d_cut < m_p + a_sh) or not is_prime_int(m_p + a_sh):\n        sys.exit(2)\n\n    qualifying_m = 0\n    selected_counts = {}\n    for m in range(X + 1, 2 * X + 1):\n        j_primes = [x for x in range(m + 1, m + h + 1) if is_prime_int(x)]\n        i_primes = [x for x in range(m + h + 1, m + 2 * h + 1) if is_prime_int(x)]\n        if j_primes and not i_primes:\n            qualifying_m += 1\n            pm = j_primes[-1]\n            selected_counts[pm] = selected_counts.get(pm, 0) + 1\n\n    gap_primes = [p for p in range(X + 1, 2 * X + h + 1) if is_prime_int(p) and next_prime_int(p) - p > h]\n    max_mult = max(selected_counts.values()) if selected_counts else 0\n\n    primes = []\n    cand_p = 2\n    while len(primes) < n_ratios + 1:\n        if is_prime_int(cand_p):\n            primes.append(cand_p)\n        cand_p += 1\n\n    ratio_inc_count = 0\n    ratio_gap_match = True\n    for idx in range(1, n_ratios + 1):\n        pn = primes[idx - 1]\n        pn1 = primes[idx]\n        c1 = fmpq(pn, idx) < fmpq(pn1, idx + 1)\n        c2 = fmpq(pn1 - pn, 1) > fmpq(pn, idx)\n        if c1 != c2:\n            ratio_gap_match = False\n        if c1:\n            ratio_inc_count += 1\n\n    def f_eval(ds):\n        j = len(ds)\n        if j == 0:\n            return fmpq(1, 1)\n        prod = 1\n        for d in ds:\n            prod *= d\n        if prod > d_cut:\n            return fmpq(0, 1)\n        if j == 1:\n            return fmpq(d_cut + 1 - prod, d_cut + 1)\n        if j == 2:\n            return fmpq(d_cut + 1 - prod, (d_cut + 1) ** 2)\n        return fmpq(0, 1)\n\n    i_shifts = list(range(h + 1, 2 * h + 1))\n    z_full = fmpq(0, 1)\n    for j in range(3):\n        for sub in itertools.combinations(i_shifts, j):\n            div_lists = [sqfree_divs_mu(m_p + b) for b in sub]\n            for tup in itertools.product(*div_lists):\n                ds = [t[0] for t in tup]\n                mu_prod = 1\n                for t in tup:\n                    mu_prod *= t[1]\n                z_full += mu_prod * f_eval(ds)\n\n    rem_shifts = [b for b in i_shifts if b != a_sh]\n    z_del = fmpq(0, 1)\n    for j in range(3):\n        for sub_u in itertools.combinations(rem_shifts, j):\n            div_lists = [sqfree_divs_mu(m_p + b) for b in sub_u]\n            for tup in itertools.product(*div_lists):\n                ds = [t[0] for t in tup]\n                mu_prod = 1\n                for t in tup:\n                    mu_prod *= t[1]\n                ftilde = f_eval(ds) + f_eval(ds + [1])\n                z_del += mu_prod * ftilde\n\n    return {\n        \"gap_primes\": len(gap_primes),\n        \"max_multiplicity\": max_mult,\n        \"qualifying_m\": qualifying_m,\n        \"ratio_gap_match\": ratio_gap_match,\n        \"ratio_inc_count\": ratio_inc_count,\n        \"selected_list\": sorted(selected_counts.keys()),\n        \"selected_primes\": len(selected_counts),\n        \"z_deleted\": str(z_del),\n        \"z_full\": str(z_full),\n    }\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\n    res = solve(data)\n | software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper establishes asymptotic density lower bounds for consecutive prime gaps satisfying $h>C\log p$ and increasing ratios $p_n/n$; it provides no software specifications, event-monitoring guarantees, or operational telemetry thresholds. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform average $\mathbb E_X$ and the prime-tuple singular series $\mathfrak S(\mathcal H)$ are deterministic number-theoretic averages over dyadic integer intervals rather than stochastic simulation engines or game-theoretic models. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proofs are purely analytical arguments using smooth divisor sums, Fourier inversion, and the Bombieri-Vinogradov distribution theorem, introducing no software tools, solvers, or computer-algebra packages. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves asymptotic existence of prime gaps and sieve weights by fixing a finite dimension $k$ before sending $X\to\infty$, rather than providing a computational algorithm for finding prime gaps or sieving primes. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The adjacent intervals $(m,m+h]$ and $(m+h,m+2h]$ and the shift fibers $\mathcal M_i=\kappa^{-1}(\{i\})$ are finite mathematical sets used in asymptotic moment expansions, not memory-layout or indexing data structures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardware architectures, parallel processors, digital circuits, or memory hierarchies are studied or referenced in the paper. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although the paper proves unconditional asymptotic properties of prime gaps and prime-to-index ratios, it investigates no cryptographic primitives, key-generation algorithms, or computational hardness assumptions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no statistical learning models, neural architectures, training objectives, or data-driven inference procedures; its second-moment and Chebyshev concentration steps operate strictly on deterministic sieve profiles. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction of the one-dimensional profile $g$ and the alternating simplex-truncated family $(f_j)_{0\le j\le k}$ establishes analytic existence of functions with $w\to1$ and $v\to0$, rather than solving a numerical optimization or operations-research problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Consensus protocols, distributed state replication, fault tolerance, and network scheduling are completely absent from the paper. |
| other (analytic_number_theory) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper directly proves in analytic number theory that a positive proportion of consecutive prime gaps satisfy $h>C\log p$ for every fixed $C>0$, deduces positive lower asymptotic density for indices where $p_n/n$ increases, and develops an adjacent-interval multidimensional sieve method combining coordinate-deletion coupling, alternating-dimension cancellation, uniform mixed-moment asymptotics, and box averages of the prime-tuple singular series. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (nonconstructive): The lower-density proportion and initial-segment threshold depend on the fixed gap multiplier and hold only for sufficiently large initial segments of the prime sequence; explicit numerical formulas for the proportion constant and cutoff index are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Using the main theorem as an explicit finite-prefix prime-gap count at a specified finite index without establishing the unknown cutoff constant.']
- **L02** (formal_scope): A lower bound on the maximum prime gap or a positive proportion of prime-free integer-start intervals does not by itself imply a positive proportion of consecutive prime gaps exceeding a fixed logarithmic multiple, because a sparse set of very long gaps can cover many integer starting points. Blocked use: ['Inferring positive index density of large consecutive prime gaps directly from maximum-gap records or from unweighted empty-interval proportions.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The increasing-ratio conclusion applies strictly to successive prime-to-index ratios of the prime sequence after discarding finitely many initial indices where the ratio is at least twice the prime logarithm. Blocked use: ['Applying the positive lower-density ratio increase conclusion to arbitrary operational telemetry metrics, general integer sequences, or unverified finite initial prefixes.']
- **L04** (hypothesis): In the adjacent-interval weight construction, the detector second-moment bound is independent of the suppression tolerance, whereas the fourth-moment weight bound depends on both the interval scale parameter and the suppression tolerance. Blocked use: ['Assuming a uniform upper bound on the second moment of the normalized weights as the second-interval prime suppression tolerance tends to zero.']
- **L05** (hypothesis): The combinatorial conversion from adjacent prime-containing and prime-free intervals to distinct consecutive prime gaps requires positive integer interval length and counts primes in the extended dyadic range up to the upper endpoint plus the interval length, with each selected prime counted at most the interval length times. Blocked use: ['Treating every qualifying integer start as a distinct prime gap without dividing by the interval-length multiplicity.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): The exact prime-deletion identity for the signed divisor sum holds when the shifted argument in the second interval is prime and relies on the coordinate support cutoff being strictly less than one so that every prime divisor larger than the base scale lies outside the test-function support. Blocked use: ['Applying the coordinate-deletion bijection at composite shifted integers or with test functions whose coordinate support exceeds the logarithmic prime scale.']
- **L07** (nonconstructive): The parameter order fixes the one-dimensional profile, the cutoff parameters, and a single finite maximum dimension before taking the dyadic scale to infinity; no divisor-sum asymptotic uniform in the maximum dimension is proved or used. Blocked use: ['Letting the sieve dimension grow simultaneously with the dyadic prime scale or assuming dimension-uniform error bounds in the divisor-sum correlations.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): The uniform mixed-moment asymptotic requires the sum of the coordinate support budgets across all divisor-sum factors to be strictly less than one in the unmarked case and strictly less than one half when a prime mark is present. Blocked use: ['Evaluating mixed divisor-sum moments via the asymptotic formula when the combined support budget reaches or exceeds one unmarked or one half with a prime mark.']
- **L09** (edge_case): In the mixed-moment proposition, the explicit integral formula in terms of signed complete mixed derivatives is restricted to shift patterns where each fiber has cardinality at most two. Blocked use: ['Applying the two-fiber mixed-derivative integral formula directly to shift patterns where three or more coordinates share the same numerical shift.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The weighted prime distribution bound with shifted endpoints assumes the Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem and restricts squarefree moduli to a fixed power strictly below one half of the base scale. Blocked use: ['Applying the shifted-endpoint prime distribution error bound to moduli at or above the square root of the base scale.']
- **L11** (approximation): The box average of the prime-tuple singular series over distinct shifts holds asymptotically as the interval length tends to infinity for a fixed tuple size and diameter ratio, with a slowly decaying iterated-logarithmic error term. Blocked use: ['Treating the singular-series box mean as exact on short finite intervals without accounting for the iterated-logarithmic error term.']
- **L12** (counterexample_scope): Finite computational checks can verify the combinatorial prime-gap multiplicity bound, the exact divisor-sum deletion identity at primes, and finite moment algebra on bounded ranges, but cannot certify the asymptotic Bombieri-Vinogradov error bounds or the global lower-density theorem. Blocked use: ['Interpreting a finite oracle certificate on bounded intervals as a formal proof or numerical verification of the asymptotic prime-gap density theorem.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 026 (`lean/docs/026.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Positive lower density of large prime gaps](../../preprints/Positive-lower-density-of-large-prime-gaps-September-25-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized supplement proves that the indices $n$ for which $p_n/n<p_{n+1}/(n+1)$ have positive lower asymptotic density, where $p_n$ is the $n$th prime. Thus the normalized prime sequence has a positive-density set of increases. This is the prime-ratio corollary associated with the paper's theorem on a positive lower density of large prime gaps.

Comparator source `PrimeGaps.lean` (1201 bytes, sha256 `a8822683e840bc0f92cb3573b85a6b1d847314b869e7262c4a863b5c33858aa5`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace LargePrimeGaps

noncomputable def prime (n : ℕ) : ℕ := Nat.nth Nat.Prime (n - 1)

noncomputable def largeGapIndices (C : ℝ) (N : ℕ) : Finset ℕ := by
  classical
  exact (Finset.Icc 1 N).filter fun n =>
    C * Real.log (prime n : ℝ) < (prime (n + 1) : ℝ) - (prime n : ℝ)

end LargePrimeGaps

noncomputable section

namespace Problem344

def primeAt (n : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  Nat.nth Nat.Prime (n - 1)

def initialCount (A : Set ℕ) (N : ℕ) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.Icc 1 N).filter (fun n => n ∈ A)).card

def lowerAsymptoticDensity (A : Set ℕ) : ℝ :=
  sSup {d : ℝ | ∃ N0 : ℕ, ∀ N : ℕ, N0 ≤ N →
    d ≤ (initialCount A N : ℝ) / (N : ℝ)}

def ratioIncreaseIndices : Set ℕ :=
  {n | 1 ≤ n ∧
    (primeAt n : ℝ) / (n : ℝ) <
      (primeAt (n + 1) : ℝ) / ((n + 1 : ℕ) : ℝ)}

theorem large_gaps_and_ratio_density :
    (∀ C : ℝ, 0 < C →
      ∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ ∃ N₀ : ℕ, ∀ N : ℕ, N₀ ≤ N →
        c * (N : ℝ) ≤ ((LargePrimeGaps.largeGapIndices C N).card : ℝ)) ∧
    0 < lowerAsymptoticDensity ratioIncreaseIndices := by
  sorry

end Problem344

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed <i>C</i> &gt; 0, we prove that a positive proportion of consecutive prime gaps exceed $`C\log p`$, where <i>p</i> is the smaller prime. The proportion is bounded below for every sufficiently large initial segment of the prime sequence, with a constant depending on <i>C</i>. It follows that the indices at which $`p_n/n`$ increases have positive lower asymptotic density, answering a question of Erdős and Prachar.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| [stmt-cor-ratios](#stmt-cor-ratios) | corollary | `cor:ratios` | 2 | The set $$\left\{n\ge1:\frac{p_n}{n}<\frac{p_{n+1}}{n+1}\right\}$$ has positive lower asymptotic density.* |
